## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-21T17:43:07Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Problem (Q1): all three together. Virtuoso Drive depends on Google Cloud, anyone on the internet can change or delete its content, and with no tests, pipeline or infrastructure definition every change is manual and risky.\n- Who it is for (Q2): student readers who find and download material, contributors (students or monitors) who upload and organise, and the maintainer who runs it cheaply and safely.\n- Success (Q3): all six metrics with the targets in vision.md: zero runtime Google Cloud dependencies; no data change without sign-in; automated tests on every change; infrastructure 100% as code; 100% of files and folders preserved; 100% of source changes traceable to an approved AI-DLC Unit.\n- Why now (Q4): a learning and demonstration goal: a real migration with AWS and AI-DLC, provable from the repository.\n- Other stakeholders (Q5): nobody else; students are users but are not consulted during the work.\n- Who decides scope and priority (Q6): you decide alone.\n- Reporting (Q7): none; the AI-DLC record in the repository is the only record needed.\n- Left out of the first version (Q8): search, tags and previews; folder rename and folder download; sharing links, comments and ratings; a native mobile app; decommissioning the Firebase project.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

**Question**: - The ten open questions at the end of vision.md (access rules, content volume, live updates, Region, cost ceiling, address, look and language, file limits, deletion, personal data) are left for the later stages that need them.\n- Who counts as a contributor, and how someone becomes one, is not settled; the group itself is confirmed.\n- The "today" figures beside each metric in vision.md are treated as the starting point until checked against the code.\n- The features the first version must include were not asked about here; only what it leaves out was confirmed.\n- Telling students at cut-over was not chosen; if a read-only window is later agreed, this may need revisiting.\n\nKeep these as labelled assumptions, or turn them into questions now?
**Reply**: A. Accept assumptions
**Options**: ["A. Accept assumptions","B. Convert to follow-up questions"]

**Question**: Q1. What your documents already answer (all from technical-environment.md):\n- Who builds and runs it: one developer, JavaScript and Node.js experience, learning AWS and AI-DLC.\n- Cloud after cut-over: AWS only, no Google Cloud or Firebase dependency at runtime.\n- System of record: PostgreSQL for the folder tree and file metadata.\n- AWS account: a single dedicated account for this project, with a budget alarm before any resource is created.\n- Existing system during the work: the Firebase site stays live and unchanged, and nothing writes to it from the new system.\n- Automation today: no tests, no linter configuration, no pipeline.\n\nAre these correct?

**Options**: ["All correct","Some need changing"]

**Question**: - Documents (Q1): all correct. One developer with JavaScript and Node.js experience, learning AWS and AI-DLC; AWS only after cut-over; PostgreSQL as the system of record; one dedicated AWS account with a budget alarm before any resource is created; the Firebase site stays live and untouched during the work; no tests, linter or pipeline today.\n- Firebase access (Q2): you have owner access and confirmed it recently.\n- Content volume (Q3): large. Thousands of files, or more than 1 GB.\n- AWS account (Q4): a dedicated account exists and you have administrator access.\n- Cost ceiling (Q5): up to about US$50 a month.\n- Deadline (Q6): none.\n- Data-protection rules (Q7): no formal obligation identified; follow good practice and keep personal data to the minimum.\n- Data location (Q8): no rule that data must stay in Brazil; the lowest price matters more than closeness to the users.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

**Question**: [Product owner] Q1. Which capabilities must the first version include? vision.md lists eight: browse; download; upload (with progress and safe duplicate names); organise (create folder, rename file, delete file and folder); sign-in and permissions; verified migration of all content; delivery and operations (pipeline, infrastructure as code, logs, alarms, rollback); fixes for the five known defects.
**Reply**: All eight are must-have
**Options**: ["All eight are must-have","Only the two safety defects are must-have","Logs and alarms are nice-to-have","Not yet defined"]

**Reply**: Any signed-in user
**Options**: ["Any signed-in user","Approved contributors only","University accounts only","Not yet decided"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-21T18:02:48Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Must-haves (Q1): all eight capabilities: browse; download; upload with progress and safe duplicate names; organise (create folder, rename file, delete file and folder); sign-in and permissions; verified migration of all content; delivery and operations (pipeline, infrastructure as code, logs, alarms, rollback); fixes for the five known defects.\n- Who may change content (Q2): any signed-in user.\n- Reading (Q3): browsing and downloading stay open to everyone; sign-in is only for changing content.\n- Live list (Q4): must-have; the open folder updates by itself when someone else changes it, as today.\n- Look and language (Q5): unchanged.\n- Cut-over (Q6): a read-only period of a few hours is acceptable.\n- Order of work (Q7): foundation first: pipeline and infrastructure before any feature.\n- Accounts (Q8): anyone can sign up for themselves; knowing who made each change is accepted as protection enough.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-21T18:19:44Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Signed-out view (Q1): all five actions stay visible; choosing one that needs sign-in asks the visitor to sign in first.\n- Sign-in placement (Q2): top right corner; "Sign in" when signed out, the person's name and "Sign out" when signed in.\n- Devices and accessibility (Q3): phone and laptop, every function usable by keyboard and readable by a screen reader (WCAG 2.1 AA).\n- Duplicate upload name (Q4): ask each time: replace the existing file, keep both, or skip.\n- Delete (Q5): always confirm, naming what will be deleted; for a folder, how many files and folders are inside.\n- Who changed it (Q6, Q7): an extra "changed by" column beside name and size, showing a display name (never the email), only to signed-in visitors.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T01:30:00Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Maintainer] Q1. Do you accept the risks this phase leaves open? Five are on record: anyone can sign up and then change or delete content; every capability is must-have, so there is no smaller fallback; the live-updating list was never costed against the budget; whether the final copy fits in a few hours is unproven until a rehearsal; data-protection duties are assumed to be light.
**Reply**: Accept all five
**Options**: ["Accept all five","Accept, but revisit open sign-up","Revisit before going on","Not yet decided"]

---

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T01:30:00Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Maintainer] Q2. What happens to the eleven review points you approved as open? Two are rated major: what the "changed by" column shows for content that existed before the move, and how people on the old site learn it is read-only when the old site must stay untouched.
**Reply**: Carry all eleven forward
**Options**: ["Carry all eleven forward","Carry forward the major ones only","Drop them all","Not yet decided"]

---

**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T01:30:00Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED

**Reply**: Go
**Options**: ["Go","Go, and measure the content first","No-go for now","Not yet decided"]

**Question**: [Developer] Q5. Should a merge be refused when too little of the code is covered by tests? One of your success measures is "automated tests run on every change"; a minimum share of covered lines makes that checkable, and 80% is the usual default for work of this kind.
**Reply**: Yes, at least 80%
**Options**: ["Yes, at least 80%","Yes, a different number","No fixed number","Not yet decided"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T01:49:28Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED

**Question**: [Maintainer] Q6. After a change passes its checks in a test environment, how does it reach production? The first two options mean running two AWS environments, which has to fit the ceiling of about US$50 a month; whether it fits depends on how the database is run, which is not decided yet.
**Reply**: I approve by hand
**Options**: ["I approve by hand","Automatically","One environment until the switch","Not yet decided"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T01:49:51Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Branch (Q1): finished work merges into `aws-migration`; one merge into `master` when the AWS version is accepted.\n- Pull requests (Q2): every piece goes through a pull request, and the checks must pass before it merges.\n- Thin slice first (Q3): yes: pipeline and infrastructure plus one trivial page, one server call and one database read, deployed to AWS, before any feature.\n- When tests are written (Q4): after the code for each layer, before the piece is merged.\n- Coverage (Q5): a merge is refused below 80% of lines covered by tests.\n- Reaching production (Q6): two environments; production waits for your approval by hand.\n- Code style (not asked): strict TypeScript, ESLint and Prettier with its default settings, enforced in the pipeline.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T01:59:13Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Product owner] Q1. What may be uploaded? Today there is no limit on size or type. With open sign-up, a limit is the main protection against the storage being filled or misused, and it bears directly on the monthly cost.
**Reply**: 1 GB, any type
**Options**: ["100 MB, any type","1 GB, any type","100 MB, no programs or scripts","Not yet decided"]

---

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T01:59:13Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Product owner] Q2. Is a delete permanent? Today it is. In the new version any signed-in user can delete and anyone can sign up, so one careless or hostile person could remove a lot. A recycle period lets items be brought back; they keep costing storage until it ends.
**Reply**: Mark as deleted and keep it away from the user facing the site, but keep it stored behind the scenes. Only the maintainer can exclude permanently going directly through AWS.
**Options**: ["Permanent","30 days, maintainer restores","30 days, any signed-in user restores","Not yet decided"]

---

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T01:59:13Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Product owner] Q3. What can the maintainer do that other signed-in users cannot? The scope gives every signed-in user the same rights; with open sign-up there is so far no way to stop an account that misbehaves.
**Reply**: Disable accounts and see history
**Options**: ["Disable accounts and see history","Also only maintainer deletes folders","Nothing special","Not yet decided"]

---

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T01:59:13Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Product owner] Q4. What does "changed by" show when there is no one to name? Every file and folder brought over from the old system has no recorded author, so on day one almost every row is in this case; the same arises for an account that no longer exists.
**Reply**: Dash and Deleted account
**Options**: ["Dash and Deleted account","Before the move and Deleted account","Empty cell","Not yet decided"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T02:03:40Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Uploads (Q1): up to 1 GB per file, any type.\n- Upload ceiling (Q10): each account may upload at most 5 GB a day.\n- Delete (Q2): items are marked as deleted and hidden from the site, but stay in storage; only the maintainer removes them permanently, directly in AWS.\n- Restore (Q9): the maintainer can restore deleted items from the site.\n- Maintainer (Q3): can disable an account and see the history of who changed what; otherwise an ordinary user.\n- "Changed by" with no one to name (Q4): a dash for content brought over; "Deleted account" for an account that no longer exists.\n- Removing an account (Q5): by asking the maintainer; the person's files stay, and their name on past changes becomes "Deleted account".\n- Web address (Q6): virtuoso.andrefellipelabs.com, on your Route 53 domain andrefellipelabs.com.\n- Old site at the switch (Q7): Firebase access rules become read-only for the final copy; once the switch is accepted, the old page is replaced by a notice linking to the new address.\n- Unclean content (Q8): bring over every item with both a record and a file; list the rest in a report; delete nothing from the old system.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

**Question**: [Maintainer] Q3. At migration, what happens when two files with the same name end up in one folder? Same-named folders share one set of contents today and are brought over as one folder; the new system requires names to be unique in a folder.
**Reply**: Keep both, number the second
**Options**: ["Keep both, number the second","Keep the first, report the others","Not yet decided"]

**Question**: - Plan: four personas (reader, contributor, maintainer on the site, maintainer as operator); about 25 to 30 stories, one per journey, following the requirement groups; every story Must Have; acceptance criteria as Given / When / Then.\n- Email confirmation (Q1): required; after signing up the person opens a link sent by email, then returns to the same folder and continues.\n- Account basics (Q2): all three: reset a forgotten password by email; a minimum password length; a pause after repeated failed sign-ins.\n- Same-named files after a folder merge at migration (Q3): keep both; the second gets a numbered name, and the report notes it.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T02:31:44Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Actions (Q1): as today: Upload and New Folder always shown; Download, Rename and Delete appear when the selection allows them; signed-out visitors see the same and are asked to sign in on use.\n- Maintainer functions (Q2): a "Maintainer" link at the top right, shown only to maintainers, opening a separate screen with Accounts, Changes and Deleted items.\n- Sign-up, sign-in and password reset (Q3): built into this site, in its own look, shown over the list. This differs from the hosted pages planned in technical-environment.md, and means passwords pass through this site's own pages.\n- Duplicate names (Q4): "do the same for the other files" is offered when more than one file has a name that already exists.\n- After confirming an email address (Q5): back in the same folder, signed in, with a line reminding them what they were about to do; they choose the action again.\n- Telling accounts apart (Q6): only the maintainer sees each account's email address beside its display name.\n- Account figures (Q7): password of at least 10 characters; 15-minute pause after 5 wrong passwords in a row; confirmation and reset links work 24 hours.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Developer] Q1. How finely should the system be divided into building blocks? Finer blocks are easier to test and reason about one at a time, but there are more boundaries to learn. Coarser blocks are quicker to start with, but the largest one grows to hold most of the logic.
**Reply**: Six blocks
**Options**: ["Six blocks","Four blocks","Decide for me"]

---

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T02:38:57Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Maintainer] Q2. How long is the record of who changed what kept? It is the protection that open sign-up relies on. It holds no email addresses, only which account did what to which item and when, and it is small.
**Reply**: Indefinitely
**Options**: ["Indefinitely","One year","90 days","Not yet decided"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T02:46:41Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Pieces (Q1): nine: the thin slice with pipeline and operations; accounts; browsing; downloading; the record of changes with live updates; organising (folders, rename, delete, restore); uploading; the migration tool; the switch.\n- Deployment (settled earlier): one application; the six building blocks stay separate inside it.\n- Working rhythm (settled earlier): one piece at a time, through a pull request; the thin slice first; the order of the rest is decided at Delivery Planning.\n- Which piece needs which: accounts and browsing need the thin slice; the record of changes needs accounts and browsing; downloading needs browsing; organising needs accounts, browsing and the record of changes; uploading needs downloading and organising; the migration tool needs browsing and downloading; the switch needs everything.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T02:52:45Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: Q1. What your documents already answer (all from technical-environment.md):\n- Style: REST over HTTPS, exchanging JSON.\n- Versions: by the path, starting at /v1/.\n- Documentation: an OpenAPI 3 description kept in the repository and checked in the pipeline.\n- Naming: paths in kebab-case, JSON fields in camelCase.\n- Errors: the Problem Details format.\n- File contents: straight between the browser and storage through short-lived links issued by the server, never through the server itself.\n\nAre these correct?
**Reply**: All correct
**Options**: ["All correct","Some need changing"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T02:52:45Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Developer] Q2. Who is the interface for? If only this site's own pages use it, the page and the server always change together and nothing outside can break. If others may build on it, every change must stay compatible with programs you do not control.
**Reply**: Only this site
**Options**: ["Only this site","Also open to others","Not yet decided"]

---

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T02:52:45Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Developer] Q3. How does the open folder learn that someone changed it? It must update within 5 seconds. Asking repeatedly is simple to build and test, and each open page costs one small request every few seconds. A pushed message arrives at once and costs nothing while nothing changes, but needs a standing connection, which is more to build and has more ways to fail.
**Reply**: Decide for me
**Options**: ["The page asks every 3 seconds","The server pushes a message","Decide for me"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T03:02:04Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Maintainer] Q1. After the thin slice, what comes first: the migration, or the rest of the site? The migration tool needs only browsing and downloading. It is the largest piece with the most unknowns. Early: you learn the amount and state of the content early and get a safe copy while the old site can still be damaged. Late: a complete site is ready sooner, and the migration is written against rules that have stopped changing.
**Reply**: Migration early
**Options**: ["Migration early","Site first","Decide for me"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T03:02:04Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T03:02:04Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Developer] Q3. How does a finished Unit get merged? Your rule is a pull request on GitHub whose checks must pass. Until U1 exists there are no checks, so U1 is a special case either way.
**Reply**: Pull request for every Unit
**Options**: ["Pull request for every Unit","U1 merged locally, then pull requests","Decide inside U1"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T03:02:04Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Developer] Q4. When is the check that proves each finished Unit chosen? One command is run after each Unit to prove it works before you approve it. The repository has no tests and no such command today; U1 creates one.
**Reply**: Choose at the first checkpoint

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T03:02:30Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Order (Q1): migration early: thin slice (U1), browsing (U3), downloading (U4), migration tool (U8), then accounts (U2), record of changes (U5), organising (U6), uploading (U7), and the switch (U9).\n- Biggest worry (Q2): the work stalling before it is finished. Each Bolt is kept to one Unit, ends in something you can see working, and the plan names what to cut or pause if a Bolt drags.\n- Merging (Q3): every Unit, U1 included, is pushed as a branch and merged through a pull request that you merge on GitHub; the checks U1 creates run on its own pull request.\n- The check for each finished Unit (Q4): chosen at the first checkpoint, once U1 has created a real command.\n- Settled earlier: one Unit per Bolt; one Bolt at a time; the thin slice first; no deadline.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T03:37:47Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - The page (Q1): the site's name and logo, one line saying the server and database answered, and the version and environment.\n- Getting in (Q2): one shared password per environment, asked for by the browser; the pipeline holds the same secret; removed from production at the switch.\n- Going back (Q3): compatible database changes only, so going back never touches the database; the pipeline tests the previous version against the new structure.\n- Coverage (Q4): at least 80% of all new code as one figure; the old application is not counted.\n- Not asked, my choice: the one database read is a single "site information" record created by the first database change, which proves database changes are applied.\n- Not asked, my choice: the health check is reachable without the password and says only "ok" or a failure.\n- Not asked, my choice: the address for alarm emails is given when the one-time setup is run and is never written in the repository.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

**Question**: [Developer] Q9. Terraform itself, or OpenTofu? Since 2023 Terraform is under the Business Source License, which is not among the licences your planning document allows without approval (MIT, Apache 2.0, BSD, ISC). It is free to use for a project like this. OpenTofu is the community continuation under MPL 2.0 and reads the same files.
**Reply**: Terraform
**Options**: ["Terraform","OpenTofu"]

**Reply**: A bucket in your AWS account (Recommended)

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T03:51:48Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Region (Q1): us-east-1.\n- Server side (Q2): API Gateway (HTTP API) with Lambda.\n- Page (Q3): React with Vite; React and React DOM (MIT) are thereby approved as new libraries.\n- Infrastructure as code (Q4, Q9): Terraform, its Business Source License approved as an exception; its files are in Terraform's own language, an accepted exception to "new code is TypeScript".\n- Terraform's state (Q10): a private, encrypted, versioned bucket made by the one-time setup, which now makes three things in order: budget alarm, pipeline sign-in link, this bucket.\n- Infrastructure security scan (Q11): Checkov (Apache 2.0), approved as a new tool.\n- Pipeline (Q5): GitHub Actions.\n- Repository (Q6): new code beside the old; app/ left alone apart from removing its committed node_modules.\n- PostgreSQL service (Q7): chosen at Infrastructure Design with real prices.\n- Error-rate email (Q8): only when at least 20 requests arrived in 5 minutes and more than 5% failed; this amends requirement FR12.6.\n- Not asked, my choice: the slice's call answers within 1 second for 95 of 100 requests once warm, and within 5 seconds after a period of no use; the page is usable within 3 seconds on a phone connection.\n- Not asked, from your planning document: Powertools for AWS Lambda, pg and Zod.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

**Question**: [Maintainer] Q1. How is the access password asked for? You chose 'asked for by the browser'. But the browser's own password box sends the password in the same place where signed-in sessions will travel from Bolt 5, so once accounts exist the two would collide.
**Reply**: A password page (Recommended)

**Recommended**: ["A password page"]

**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T04:02:48Z

**Question**: [Developer] Q2. Which tool applies changes to the database structure? Changes are plain SQL files in the repository, applied in order by the pipeline and recorded so none is applied twice. Any new tool needs your approval.
**Reply**: node-pg-migrate (Recommended)

**Recommended**: ["node-pg-migrate"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T04:06:39Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Access password (Q1): a small password page of the site's own; the device remembers it for 30 days in a cookie, which never touches the place sessions use. This replaces "asked for by the browser".\n- Database changes (Q2): node-pg-migrate (MIT), approved as a new library, applying plain SQL files.\n- Not asked, my choice: the page and the server share one address, as the contract already says; calls to the server pass through the content delivery network, so the access password is checked in one place for both.\n- Not asked, my choice: the check at the network's edge holds only a scrambled form of the password, never the password itself.\n- Not asked, my choice: database changes are applied by a small function running next to the database, which the pipeline starts; the pipeline never connects to the database itself.\n- Not asked, my choice: the server is not kept warm; the first request after a quiet period is slower, within the 5 seconds already agreed.\n- Not asked, a limit stated plainly: the 50-requests-a-second limit protects the server. The page's static files have no such limit without a paid protective service, which is not included.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T04:28:06Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Maintainer] Q1. Which PostgreSQL service, and how many? The database is the largest fixed cost. Aurora Serverless only saves money by pausing when idle, and production can't pause because the health check asks the database every minute.
**Reply**: RDS, one per environment (Recommended)
**Options**: ["RDS, one per environment (Recommended)","One RDS instance, shared","Aurora Serverless"]
**Recommended**: ["RDS, one per environment"]

---

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T04:28:06Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Developer] Q2. How does the server get its secret values? It sits in a private network with no internet route. Your requirement says secrets are 'read by the server when it starts' from the secrets service, which from there needs a paid private connection point.
**Reply**: Private connection points
**Options**: ["Hand over at deployment (Recommended)","Private connection points"]
**Recommended**: ["Hand over at deployment"]

---

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T04:42:50Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: - Database (Q1): Amazon RDS for PostgreSQL, db.t4g.micro with 20 GB, one in each environment, always on.\n- Secrets (Q2): a private connection point to the secrets service in each environment; the server reads its secrets when it starts.\n- Ceiling (Q3): raised to US$60 a month; the budget alarm emails at US$48 and US$60. This changes two requirements. The estimate is US$47.50 to US$50.20 at expected use.\n- Not asked, my choice: a private network across two of the Region's sites, with no internet route and no public addresses; the one connection point sits in one site.\n- Not asked, my choice: the server and the change runner sign in to the database with their own AWS identities, so neither has a database password. RDS keeps the master password in the secrets service; it is used once, to create those two sign-ins.\n- Not asked, my choice: the access password is kept in the free parameter store, since only you and the pipeline read it; the secret the edge adds for the server is in the secrets service.\n- Not asked, my choice: the edge check is a CloudFront Function; the health monitor is a Route 53 health check; at most ten custom measurements, to stay within what is free.\n- Not verified: where the edge function's log lines are written, and the size limit of the password page it answers with. Both are checked when the code is planned.\n\nDoes this all look correct before I generate the artifact?
**Reply**: Looks correct
**Options**: ["Looks correct","Request changes"]

**Reply**: Only this project
**Options**: ["Only this project","The whole account","The account is dedicated"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T22:35:37Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Developer] Q2. May aws-migration become the repository's default branch? GitHub starts hand-started workflows only from the default branch, and the going-back workflow is hand-started. master itself is not changed.
**Reply**: Yes (Recommended)
**Options**: ["Yes (Recommended)","No, keep master"]
**Recommended**: ["Yes"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T22:35:37Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Developer] Q3. Do you approve these eight libraries and tools? Bootstrap bundled (MIT); Terraform's AWS provider (MPL 2.0); Vite's React plugin (MIT); eslint-plugin-react and react-hooks (MIT); axe-core with its Playwright integration for the accessibility scan (MPL 2.0); Gitleaks secret scanner (MIT); React Testing Library with jsdom (MIT); esbuild for bundling the server and edge check (MIT).
**Reply**: Approve all eight
**Options**: ["Approve all eight","Approve all but some","Approve none yet"]

---

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-25T22:35:37Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: [Maintainer] Q4. Three smaller points from the design: (1) the notification topic is created a moment before the budget alarm, so the email address is held only by the notification service; (2) slowing repeated wrong access passwords is withdrawn, since the password's length makes guessing infeasible; (3) the test environment's address is test.virtuoso.andrefellipelabs.com. Accept all, or go one by one?
**Reply**: Accept all three
**Options**: ["Accept all three","One by one"]

## Question Replied
**Timestamp**: 2026-11-26T01:36:15Z
**Event**: QUESTION_REPLIED
**Session**: f2fa31c6-27c7-4550-8a6a-b10786a81641
**Question**: Do you accept the seven accessibility changes to the look? (1) check mark on selected rows, (2) taller progress bar, (3) visible focus ring, (4) text selectable again, (5) site-styled dialogs replace browser boxes, (6) action links become same-looking buttons, (7) darker blue primary buttons. Returning students would notice 1, 2 and 5.
**Reply**: Accept all seven (Recommended)
**Options**: ["Accept all seven (Recommended)","Accept the five U3 builds"]
**Recommended**: ["Accept all seven"]
